# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

### 💵 DATA SUBSCRIPTIONS — what we pay for and why (answered 2026-09-02, measured not assumed)

Operator asked *"what are we paying for and why, what are the alternatives"* and then *"check it, I
don't want to add another sub, find a good solution here."* **Answer: nothing is duplicated, and the
$99 line is load-bearing.**

| Line | Cost | What it buys | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Alpaca Algo Trader Plus** (`ALPACA_DATA_FEED=sip`) | ~$99/mo | The real-time consolidated tape. Every EP number — gap %, RVOL, ORB high/low — is computed off these bars at 9:31–9:45 ET | **KEEP — proven not redundant** |
| **Polygon** | monthly | Grouped daily across ~12,500 tickers (the RS universe), news, splits, ticker reference | Keep for now; see the one real option below |
| **Anthropic** | $47.91 / 30d | Sonnet 5 $33.07 (2,987 calls) · Opus 5 $9.25 · Sonnet 4.6 $3.59 · Haiku $2.00 | metered |
| **Perplexity** | $21.90 / 30d, **$0.99 in Sept** | Second-opinion grade + overnight brief. The Agent-API migration (#603) collapsed the cost | metered, already fixed |
| FMP · yfinance · SEC EDGAR | **$0** | Analyst estimates (annual), fundamentals, filing-date anchors | free tier is enough |

🔑 **THE $99 IS NOT REDUNDANT — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** Polygon on our current plan DOES return
minute and second aggregates, which is what made the duplication worth testing. But today's bars
come back stamped **`status: DELAYED`**, and `/v3/trades` returns **403 NOT_AUTHORIZED — "You are
not entitled to this data"**. A 15-minute delay is unusable for the ORB window: we would be placing
9:31 entries against 9:16 prices. Making Polygon real-time means UPGRADING Polygon, which is adding
cost, not saving it.

▶ **THE ONE REAL OPTION, and it is a migration rather than a click:** everything we use Polygon for
— the daily universe, news, splits, ticker reference — is DELAY-TOLERANT, and Alpaca (already paid,
already real-time) serves all of it, including news via its Benzinga feed which
`news_source_quality.py` already names. **So the candidate saving is dropping POLYGON, not Alpaca.**
Not scoped, not costed, not started — recorded so the question is not re-asked from scratch.

**STRETCH, in the order to take them** (operator 2026-09-02: *"include a few stretch items as well
if we get to it"*):

| # | Plain name | Why it earns a stretch slot |
|---|---|---|
| **#482** | bracket geometry — our stop shakes out winners | Same replay harness as #545, so marginal cost is small once that card runs |
| **#210** | an accurate read of the news | Its own re-scope says the first work lands when capacity resets — that is now |
| **#593** | the +20% revert trigger fired on an artifact | Predicate wiring is mechanical; the settled-close basis change is a live admission-gate definition → operator sign-off |
| **#255** | judge precedent retrieval | Blocked on "enough corpus" with NO number. Name the count that makes it testable, or un-block and close — the line itself says re-dating is the dishonest option |
| **#261** | scripts/ namespace tidy | Pure burndown, mechanical, no date pressure |

⚠ **Why this block lives in PLAN.md and not in chat:** the sequence and the below-70 target are the
operator's, and chat compacts. If the week is re-sequenced, EDIT this block — do not leave it
asserting a plan that is no longer being run.

The claim is that a provisional number is now the biggest filter in the detector and has never been evaluated. ▶ **THE WORK:** replay the 3.0 / 3.5 / 4.0% variants over the stored candidate history, and read BOTH directions — real breakouts recovered vs junk admitted — before proposing anything. ⚖ **THE LINE: `_HTF_MIN_ADR_PCT` is a DETECTION CRITERION. [Operational safety / hardening] ✅ **DIAGNOSED + FIXED 2026-08-29 — `docs/analysis/595_missed_outcomes_anchor_2026-08-29.md`. THE ARITHMETIC WAS NEVER WRONG; THE PREMISE WAS.** VEEE 07-08: the SCAN saw 16.5–20.8% (pre-market), the stock **OPENED at +4.1%** (prior close 5.63 → open 5.86) and **closed −21%**; the forward window ran from that open and swept up an unrelated 07-13 move (open 12.24 vs a 4.82 close) as +354%. So the anchor is not a wrong DATE — it is a pre-market print that faded before the bell, on a day that was never a setup. 📊 **SCALE: 2,654 of 4,022 rows (66%) never gapped at the open, and 203 of the 331 ranked "big winners" (61%).** Worst: VEEE +354% on a +4.1% open · XNDU +194% · POEL +193% · HQ +187% · CUE +172% on a +1.2% open · **JLHL +114% on a −3.4% open — it opened DOWN and was ranked as a winner we missed.** ✅ **FIX (same test the operator applied to #593's sustain rule two days earlier — must be theoretically tradeable before it counts):** `open_gap_pct` + `setup_at_open` written on every row from the prior session's close (STRICTLY earlier — no lookahead) against `MIN_GAP_PCT` **imported** from `ep_detector`, never restated, so it cannot answer by the obsolete 10.0 floor. **The row is still WRITTEN** — a faded pre-market print is real telemetry about our own scan and suppressing it is the same mistake reversed — it just **no longer RANKS**: `/scanned` prints what the name did, then *"but no setup at the open (+4%), not ranked"*. **NULL = not computed** (every pre-existing row) and keeps ranking as before; treating NULL as "no setup" would have silently emptied the rankings on ship, and a mutation test pins exactly that. 6 tests, both mutations caught. Suite 6519 green. ⚠ **WHICH PAST CONCLUSIONS THIS INVALIDATES — measured per gate, 2026-08-29** (26 docs read this table; each argues one gate, so the corruption rate BY SKIP CATEGORY is the usable map): **pm_rvol_low 86% fake · catalyst_downgrade 83% · cooldown 75% · adv_low 57% · high_unentered 56% · score_below_50 46% · extension_gate 43% · atr_high 33% · window_missed 33% · duplicate_scan 19% · ma_filter 0%.** 🔑 **The pattern is NOT random — the three worst are gates that fire EARLY on pre-market information**, so their "victims" are exactly the prints that faded and were never tradeable. **Concretely at risk:** `adv_floor_556_2026-08-20` (57% fake, largest claimed-winner base) · `cooldown_cost_557_2026-08-21` + `cooldown_60d_effectiveness_2026-07-26` (75%) · `gates_extension_top20_577_2026-08-22` + `conviction_floor_extension_2026-08-03` (43%) · `orb_window_587_2026-08-23` (33%, base of 6). ✅ **NOT at risk: anything reasoning over ALERTS rather than skips** — an alert gapped by definition, so this filter cannot move it; the judge, rubric and score-separation work is unaffected. ✅ **FIRST RE-RUN DONE 2026-08-29 — the EXTENSION CAP, because it is the ONLY live change any of these analyses produced** (50%→75%, operator-signed 08-21, the second admission loosening ever): `docs/analysis/577_extension_cap_recheck_2026-08-29.md`. **The headline it rested on — 21 winners the gate cost us — corrects to 12; nine were never setups.** 🔑 **BUT THE REAL FINDING IS NOT THE INFLATION: only 3 of the 12 real winners are in the 50-75% band the change actually admits.** Nine had run **128%-2,264%** in the prior five days and are STILL blocked at 75% — including the three biggest (+286%, +172%, +131%). The change recovers **3 names in four months**. 📉 **AND THE BAND IT OPENS IS BAD: 17 real setups, 3 big winners, 4 up vs 13 down, median −21.7%.** Above 75% is no different (n=74, median −33.0%), and the whole blocked cohort runs 18 up / 69 down, median −31.3% — the gate is not mis-calibrated at 50, the extended cohort is bad at every level. ⚖ **THE LINE — admission is entry discipline. Reverting to 50% is his call and is NOT proposed; this puts the corrected numbers in front of him.** ⚠ Returns are 5-day moves from the open, not R — the original doc's own caveat stands (MXL 04-24, the one minute-bar-checkable winner, filled at −1R). 🔬 **EXPECTANCY ANSWERED ON REAL MINUTE BARS (operator pushed back on the win-rate framing: *"more important is the expected return... is that outcome positive"* — he is right, and the table cannot answer it because its returns carry NO STOP).** Fetched the cohort from Alpaca (only 2 of 17 had bars stored) and replayed the LIVE bracket — entry = stop-buy at the 9:30 bar's high, stop = that bar's low, walked minute-by-minute IN SEQUENCE with the +2R half-off rule. **RESULT: −0.71R per trade** (14 triggered+scored, 3 never traded through; 2 winners at +1.00R, 12 losers at −1.00R). **The band is not worth admitting, and that verdict does not depend on win rate at all.** 🔑 **BUT THE REASON IS NOT THAT THESE NAMES DON'T MOVE — five ran 2.9R to 15.2R and still paid −1R** (AKTX +15.2R → −1R · HCAI +5.3R → −1R · WYHG +3.4R · ERNA +3.3R · BRUNW +2.9R), because the 9:30 bar's low was taken out first. **That is a GEOMETRY finding, not an admission one** — the same conclusion #482/#468b reached independently (*"a 1-min-range stop shakes out winners"*) and the same caveat the original 08-21 doc carried (*conversion, not admission, decides whether a recovered winner pays*). **Loosening the cap admits names our stop cannot hold.** ⚠ **THREE METHOD ERRORS CAUGHT AND CORRECTED BEFORE THIS NUMBER:** the first pass let the stop lapse after day 0 (losers averaged an impossible −1.43R); the second ignored ORDER, scoring a name that ran +3R at 10:00 and drifted back at 15:00 as a flat −1R (a suspicious 14-of-14 stop-out); n=14 is small and two winners is two names. ✅ **ALL FOUR RE-RUNS COMPLETE 2026-08-29, each verified against prod independently before being accepted.** **ADV floor** (`556_adv_floor_recheck`): 28→**12** real winners; real-setup cohort is WORSE than the raw baseline (median −5.7% vs −1.9%), so the KEEP ruling holds and strengthens — removing phantom rows removed a false credit rather than manufacturing a case. Its Results 3+4 rest on the corrupted base and are flagged NOT re-validated; Result 5 (gate ordering) is structural and unaffected. **Cooldown** (`557_cooldown_recheck`): 12→**3** real winners; corrected cohort skews MORE negative (mean −4.5%, median −5.5%, n=47); both prior don't-loosen verdicts hold. **ORB window** (`587_orb_window_recheck`): 6→**4**; **n=4 is too few to found anything on and the card said so** — on the original's 25-name cohort the direction survives (62% green, median +11.8%, n=13) but one of its two marquee winners, BLZE +78%, is FAKE (TSAT +35% is real). That doc made no recommendation, so nothing reverses. 🔑 **NET: three of four rulings were KEEP and all three strengthen. The extension cap is the only one that moved live behaviour, and its case got weaker.** ✅ **REVERTED 75% → 50% 2026-08-29, OPERATOR-SIGNED (*"go with rec"*)** — `MAX_EXTENSION_PCT = 50.0`, CHANGE_PROCESS entry + gate-provenance registry updated in the same commit, three gates caught the change correctly (pinned threshold, provenance registry, silent-swallow) and were fixed rather than bypassed. Suite 6525 green. 🔭 **AND THE RE-OPEN TRIGGERS HE NAMED ARE NOW WATCHED, not left to memory** (*"we'll review this again in future when we have more samples; also if/when we miss a real strong EP because of this"*): `missed_outcomes.check_extension_cap_revisit` runs in the nightly job and (a) logs when the 50-75 band passes 40 scoreable names (15 at the revert), and (b) **TELEGRAMS per-name when a REAL setup blocked by this cap then doubles within 5 sessions** — gated on `setup_at_open` so it cannot fire on the pre-market fades that caused this whole mess, and set at +100% so it stays rare enough to still mean something. ▶ **THE REAL LEVER IS #482 (bracket geometry), not this constant** — five band names ran 2.9R-15.2R and every one still paid −1R because the 9:30 bar's low went first. Flagged, NOT corrected. ✅ **DEPLOYED + VERIFIED 2026-08-29** (Sat, ungated): both scopes exit 0, refresh run (1,534 scan_filter + 21 moderate + 69 high_unentered). ⚠ **THE REFRESH ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH — caught because the stated verify FAILED.** It rebuilds only a 30-day window, so it computed 1,623 of 4,022 rows and VEEE 07-08 (the operator's own case) still read NULL; the ~2,400-row back-catalogue would have stayed uncorrected FOREVER and kept ranking. **One-off backfill run** (same rule: prior close strictly earlier, 9% floor; touches only NULL rows, never overwrites a refresh-written value) — `UPDATE 2397`. **Prod now: 4,020 of 4,022 computed · 2,649 NOT A SETUP · 197 of the 331 ranked winners (60%) dropped.** VEEE 07-08 reads `setup_at_open=false, open_gap_pct=0.041` ✓. ▶ **ORIGINAL VERIFY-LIVE (met) = after the refresh, `setup_at_open` populated (not all-NULL) and VEEE 2026-07-08 reads `setup_at_open=false, open_gap_pct≈0.041`; `mi_ep_missed_outcomes` shows `setup_at_open` populated (not all-NULL) and VEEE 2026-07-08 reads `setup_at_open=false, open_gap_pct≈0.041`; `/scanned` on a day holding such a name prints "no setup at the open" and does not rank it.** ▶ **THEN: re-run the 9:45-window and extension-cap reads against the corrected table.** [ok:2026-08-29→2026-09-05 — fix shipped today; the new date carries the re-check of the two conclusions it invalidates, not a roll.]

## Judge & catalyst — v1.1 program
- #615 | 2026-09-08 | deployed | 🟠 **The judge was told a blanket "no" for TWO signals on every grade — both wired 2026-09-01.** `has_direct_source` was never passed by `ep_detector`'s `assemble_judge_inputs(...)` call, so it defaulted to `None` and `_b()` rendered it "no" while the rubric treats a "no" as grounds to *"prefer the floor tier"* — 97 of 99 assessable rows HAD a primary source. `revenue_stage` had the same omission, found by the new guard within an hour; it exists to stop the rubric judging a pre-revenue biotech by revenue-growth criteria (IMVT 2026-05-21) and every company alive was being presented as pre-revenue. ✅ Wired HONESTLY: revenue_stage is a TRI-STATE — `is_revenue_stage` only runs on earnings day and the boost gate's `True` elsewhere means *do not block*, NOT *has revenue*, so non-earnings rows render **"not checked"** rather than a false "no". 🔒 **SYSTEMIC FIX** (he asked why a live-path bug took a month to surface): nothing FAILS when a judge is merely misinformed, so only a report recomputing the truth could see it. `tests/test_judge_payload_completeness.py` now fails the build when a renderable signal is neither passed nor DECLARED with a reason — **detection moved from 30 days to commit time, and it found the second instance immediately.** 📊 **Expect a SMALL effect**: 78 of the last 101 grades already sit in the top two grades despite both false flags, and the judge's rationales cite the primary source and apply revenue rule 4 regardless — it reads the corpus, not our flags. ▶ **MONITORING**: every grade writes a `judge_signal_trace` row (held-vs-sent, both signals), and `judge_signal_wiring_effect_2026_09_01` in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` fires at 40 post-fix grades (~10-15) to compare the mix against the pre-fix baseline. **VERIFY-LIVE 2026-09-02: a trace row appears on the first graded alert carrying a non-'no' value.** ⚖ Bug fixes, not criteria changes — rubric, tool schema and normalizer untouched. [Judge & catalyst — v1.1 program] >> ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-09-01 21:24 ET** (all three services; both scopes + execution). VERIFY 09-08 (no alerts fired 09-02, so nothing was graded): a `judge_signal_trace` row on the first graded alert carrying a non-'no' value. ⚠ **FILTER `log_caller = 'ep_grade_judge'` when you read that surface** — `grade_holistic` has SEVEN callers (the live scan ~3/day, the chart-axis shadow job up to ~48/day, judge_divergence ~2-5/day, four offline eval/replay scripts), and every one of them now writes a trace row. The caller is stamped on each row (2026-09-02); before that stamp a single historical backfill replay would have written hundreds of rows indistinguishable from live grades. Reading unfiltered answers a different question than the one this verify asks. >> ⚠ **A STATED DECISION, NOT A GAP (advisor review 2026-09-01).** The `revenue_stage` tri-state puts a NEW STRING — `"not checked"` — into a LIVE judge prompt, on most graded rows (every non-earnings day), **and the rubric defines no behaviour for that value.** My test asserts the string RENDERS; it does not assert the judge does anything sensible with it. ⚠ **The judge-eval regression gate passed at deploy but does NOT cover this**: its baseline was recorded 2026-08-27, before the change, and it compares the GRADE SURFACE, not the prompt text. So the only thing that would catch "the judge reads *not checked* as a negative" is the `judge_signal_wiring_effect_2026_09_01` review at 40 grades (~mid-October) — **a six-week window where a prompt change to a money-path grader goes unobserved.** ▶ **ACCEPTED DELIBERATELY, with the reasoning recorded so it is a decision and not an oversight:** the alternative was leaving the judge told a definitive "no" on every non-earnings row, which is a KNOWN falsehood; "not checked" is at worst an unmodelled token and at best the truth. ▶ **SHORTEN THE WINDOW rather than wait: the `judge_signal_trace` rows already record held-vs-sent per grade, so the 09-02 verify should ALSO eyeball the first few grades' tier vs the pre-fix mix** (game_changer 35 · strong 43 · routine 21 over the prior 30 days) — a visible slide toward routine is the early signal, and does not need 40 grades to notice. ⚖ Reverting is one argument at one line if it looks wrong. >> ⏳ **09-02: NOTHING TO VERIFY — the judge graded nothing today.** Zero live EP alerts on 2026-09-02 (the briefing's *"None this morning, 4 gap candidates scanned"*, with the regime bar at **75 — correcting, exceptional only**), so no alert reached the judge and no `judge_signal_trace` row exists. **Not a defect: the trace fires per GRADE, and there were none.** EVENT-GATED on the next graded alert. ⚠ When it lands, ALSO eyeball the first grades' tier mix against the pre-fix baseline (game_changer 35 · strong 43 · routine 21 over the 30 days to 09-01) — the advisor's point that a prompt change to a money-path grader is otherwise unobserved for six weeks. [ok:2026-09-02→2026-09-08 — event-gated on the next graded alert; verified today that the scan ran and produced zero alerts, so nothing could be graded] [b1]
- #533 | 2026-09-14 | deployed | 🎯 **NOTHING measures the question the operator actually cares about: given 10 alerts this morning and 5 slots, did we take the right ones?** Operator 2026-08-05, on the earnings-season surge: *"does it capture the main goal of selecting best EPs in a given day when there's many?"* — checked, and **no**. #448 asks whether the rubric downgrades losers more than winners POOLED ACROSS DAYS; #368 weights one input. Neither is a WITHIN-DAY ranking question, and a pooled metric cannot answer it: the choice is always between the alerts of one morning. **WORSE, THE ENTRY PATH HAS NO RANKING AT ALL.** `live_tracker.process_new_alerts_live` selects with `ORDER BY ticker, ep_score DESC` — the `DISTINCT ON (ticker)` forces ticker first, so the surviving order is **ALPHABETICAL**. Which alerts get the 5 slots is therefore decided by ticker name and ORB trigger timing, not quality. Measured on 2026-08-04 (10 HIGH): the two cap-blocked names were **AEIS** (first alphabetically) and **ZBRA** (last). The only ranking that exists — `briefing._ep_composite_key` (ep_score + theme bonus + RS-rank bonus) — orders the morning BRIEFING for reading and never touches entry. **✅ NO CAPTURE FIX IS NEEDED — verified, correcting my own claim that an unranked day is unrecoverable:** every composite input is dated and retained (`mi_ep_alerts.ep_score`; `mi_themes` back to 2026-03-19, 97 days; `mi_stock_scores` back to 2025-12-26, 111 days), so any past day's ranking is reconstructible offline at $0. **DoD (read-only analysis first):** for every multi-alert day, rank that day's HIGH alerts by (a) ep_score alone and (b) the composite, join `mi_ep_scan_outcomes`, and report whether the top-N of a day out-performs the rest OF THAT SAME DAY — rank correlation + top-N-vs-rest edge, never pooled. Then a keep/change recommendation. ⚠ **THE LINE: changing what the entry path SELECTS is entry discipline and is the operator's call** — this task produces evidence and a proposal, and flips nothing. ⚠ Needs multi-alert days to mean anything (a 2-alert day has no choice in it), which is why the current earnings window matters — see the cohort note on #448; the 2026-08-04 cohort settles ~08-11. 💡 First data point, not a finding: on 08-04 PLTR had the top ep_score (96) and is the only alert still holding a position. >> 🔴 **2026-08-11 IS THE WORKED CASE THIS TASK WAS WAITING FOR — and our within-day ranking was INVERTED.** The operator flagged it live: *"we had 3 EPs today, and SE is the one that wasn't entered where in fact, it's the one i would've monitored for entry myself"* and *"the ranking within same day EP is important and this may be a good data point"*. **THE FULL BOARD, all four names, same session:** `RIOT ep=115.2 gap=17.9% RS=32.6 rank=1641 above-all-MAs=NO -> order_placed` · `FRMI ep=96 gap=17.0% RS=31.7 rank=1661 above-all-MAs=NO -> STOPPED -$31.49 at 09:31:40` · `BW ep=96 gap=34.9% RS=1.5 rank=2397 above-all-MAs=NO -> STOPPED -$49.60 at 09:31:56` · **`SE ep=54.7 gap=10.6% RS=88.5 rank=281 above-all-MAs=YES -> SKIPPED`** (`setup:gap_below_floor: rt 9.2% < 10% floor`; reclaimed to +10.5% by 09:35 and $127.94 by 09:42). 🔬 **THE SEPARATION IS TOTAL AND IT RUNS THE WRONG WAY:** our EP score ranked SE **LAST of four**, and SE is the ONLY name with any underlying strength — the only one above all three moving averages, the only one inside the top 300 by RS, and the only one with a sector. The three we scored HIGHEST (96-115) sit at **RS ranks 1641, 1661 and 2397** — BW at RS 1.5 is close to the weakest stock in the universe and gapped 34.9%. Both entered names died inside 60 seconds. ⚠ **MECHANISM, stated as a hypothesis not a finding:** the score appears to reward GAP SIZE + catalyst grade and to carry no term for trend context or underlying strength, so a dead stock gapping huge outranks a leader gapping modestly. ⚠ **N=1 SESSION, 4 NAMES — this is a worked example, NOT evidence.** It is exactly the shape this task exists to measure, so measure it across the cohort before anyone proposes a score change. ⚠ Any change to EP scoring is a detection-criterion change = CHANGE_PROCESS + N>=10 + operator sign-off. ▶ Feeds #562 (the operator's own four conditions for SE are in `docs/methodology/operator_shared_notes.md` 2026-08-11) and #563 (SE, RS 88.5 and in a strong retail group per the operator, is in NO live theme). >> 🔴🔴 **THE GRADE HAS NO CONVICTION LEFT IN IT — MEASURED 2026-08-11, 60 days, live account.** Operator: *"we should reserve HIGH/gamechanger to absolute best, not just anything that gaps up though that remains a key criteria... during earnings season, we basically buy every day because there are gap up and downs everyday, lots of randomness."* **The data says he is right on both halves.** 📊 **GRADE DISTRIBUTION (60d, live):** `game_changer` **48 of 81 alerts = 59%** — the top grade is the MODAL grade, not the exception · `strong` 27 · `routine` 6. A label awarded to the majority carries no information. 📉 **AND THE OUTCOME IS THE SAME ON BOTH TOP GRADES:** game_changer 14 closed, avg **-$22.54**, **0 winners**; strong 5 closed, avg -$20.14, **0 winners**. **ACROSS ALL 19 CLOSED LIVE TRADES IN 60 DAYS: 0 winners, 19 losers, -$416.19 total. The `source` column already existed (`yfinance` vs `fmp_stable`) and `period_type='quarter'` was already in the CHECK, so two premises in my brief were wrong and no migration was needed. ▶ **THE HARD PART WAS DATES, and the answer is measured per ticker rather than assumed:** yfinance reports RELATIVE periods (`0q` = current quarter), but a point-in-time table needs an ABSOLUTE period end or the whole premise collapses. `0q` = yfinance's next-earnings-date minus THIS ticker's own observed release lag (release compared to release on both sides); `+1q` = `0q` plus THIS ticker's own observed quarter length (EDGAR reportDate to reportDate). Rejected: a fixed 3-calendar-month step (picks the wrong MONTH on COST's real 4-4-5 retail calendar) and month-end snapping (fixes calendar filers, systematically misdates COST by 1–3 weeks). Consequence: **a quarter's label is set by whichever run FIRST sees it — often ~6 months before it reports — and held from then on.** Right for stability; it means the first estimate's date precision is what sticks. 📌 **CLARIFICATION, not a reversal:** the note that *"yfinance earnings dates were probed and REJECTED"* applies to the honest-window ANCHOR, which is still EDGAR-only and unchanged. False = we actively misinform the judge on 98% of rows (a defect, and one that would be depressing scores right now).** ⚠ Do NOT act on the 98% until that is resolved — I have been wrong three times today by asserting a cause fast, and this one touches grading. **DoD ADD: read the actual rendered judge prompt for one recent graded row and state which value it carried**, then either fix the misinformation or record that absent-is-intended. ⚖ Any grade-affecting change is CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off. >> 🔧 **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-01, and it is a WIRING GAP, not a design choice.** `ep_detector.py:5528` builds the judge payload via `assemble_judge_inputs(...)` and passes grounded_text, market_cap, sector, materiality_tier, active_narratives, setup_class and second_opinion — **but never `has_direct_source`.** So it takes the parameter's default `None` on EVERY call, and `_build_judge_prompt`'s `_b()` renders falsy as a definitive "no". The 09-01 monthly review measured it: **97 of 99 assessable rows HAD a primary source while being told there was none.** 🔑 **revenue_stage:** same omission, found by the guard below within an hour of writing it. Non-earnings rows now send `None` and render **"not checked"** via a dedicated `_b3()`. 🔒 **THE SYSTEMIC FIX, because he asked why a live-path bug took a MONTH to surface:** nothing FAILS when a judge is merely misinformed — a grade still comes out and every downstream surface looks healthy, so only a report that recomputes the truth could see it. `tests/test_judge_payload_completeness.py` now fails the build when any signal `assemble_judge_inputs` can render is neither passed nor DECLARED unwired with a reason. **Detection moved from 30 days to commit time — and it earned its keep immediately by finding the second instance.** 📊 **EXPECTATIONS, measured before shipping: probably a small effect.** 78 of the last 101 grades already sit in the top two grades despite both false flags, and the judge's own rationales cite the primary source (*"Fresh, primary-sourced catalyst exists (8-K + press release)"*) and apply revenue rule 4 (*"a thin ~1.7% revenue beat"*) regardless — **it reads the corpus rather than trusting our summary fields.** ▶ **MONITORING (operator: *"monitor its effects going forward so we are aware"*):** every grade now writes a `judge_signal_trace` row carrying held-vs-sent for both signals, and `judge_signal_wiring_effect_2026_09_01` in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` fires at 40 post-fix grades (~2026-10-15) to compare the grade mix against the recorded pre-fix baseline. - #597 | 2026-09-30 | deployed | 🐛 **If a position vanishes from the broker mid-flight, we close the row with no exit leg and book the wrong P&L.** Found 2026-08-25 by the deployed-broker review. `sync_positions`' "position gone from Alpaca" branch (`order_manager.py:5805-5816`) sets `remaining_shares=0` and closes the trade **without recording an exit leg**. It can race a #591 stays-open row: the resting exit fills at the broker, our websocket finaliser has not caught up, `sync_positions` polls in that gap, and the row closes with the fill unrecorded. **The booked P&L is then wrong and no exit leg explains it.** ✅ It is now DETECTED rather than silent — the #588 L1 invariant `exit_share_sum_mismatch` fires on exactly this shape, and its docstring's claim that the state "can no longer exist" is true only for the paths #591 touched, not for this one. ▶ **DoD: make that branch record the exit it implies (or refuse to close and let the finaliser do it), so the invariant stops being the only thing standing between a race and a wrong number.** ⚠ Detection is not correction: by the time the L1 fires, the trade's P&L is already wrong and feeds `mi_sell_discipline_records`, which every exit-rule replay reads. ⚖ **Money path — `broker/` runs on apollo-execution, needs `deploy.sh execution` as a second step.** Recording bug, not a discipline change, but a change to when a row closes is his call. Same file and class as **#596** (a partly-filled leg cancelled with no replacement) — worth doing together. [Operational safety / hardening] ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-29 (Fable card, verified inline before acceptance).** New `order_manager._resolve_position_gone`, a HYBRID of the DoD's two options because each alone re-creates a known failure: blind-close books $0 on a real loss (the bug), and refuse-only leaves a phantom open row whenever the WS event is genuinely LOST. Under the #151 per-trade advisory try-lock, re-reading the row with the `account_mode` filter: **(1) GRACE** — stop confirmed filled <10 min ago → defer, the finaliser owns it; **(2) RECORD** — older fill → book the exit from BROKER TRUTH ONLY (`filled_avg_price`/`filled_qty`/`filled_at`), exits + `total_pnl` + `remaining_shares` in ONE statement (the MNDY 2026-05-11 invariant), idempotent on `exits[].order_id`; **(3) REFUSE** — no confirmable fill → **row stays OPEN and LOUD** (audit + Telegram + a discrepancy line every sweep). ⚠ **NEVER SYNTHESISES A PRICE** — a wrong-but-confident P&L is the exact failure being fixed. ⚖ **CLOSE-TIMING CHANGED and it is declared in the code comment** (was: unconditional same-sweep close; now: same-sweep only on a confirmed fill) — recording only, no order placed/cancelled/altered, revert = delete the helper and restore the blind UPDATE. 9 tests, mutation-verified by the card (pre-fix 9/9 red; exit-leg removed, idempotency no-op'd, grace zeroed, total_pnl decoupled — all red) **and independently by me: mutating it to accept an unconfirmed fill turned 2 tests red.** ⚠ **A FOURTH GATE — `audit_column_writes` (trade-state ownership, the BW/KLAR/CRMD class) — REJECTED THE FIRST CUT with exit 6, and it was right:** the helper wrote `exits` / `remaining_shares` / `total_pnl` / `closed_at` / `status` itself, which would have made a SIXTH writer of trade-state columns. The registry's own history records that T1.3 on 2026-05-18 REMOVED a duplicate writer for exactly this reason (*"close-path delegated to finalize_stop_fill which is the canonical writer"*), so the gate's option (a) — register the new writer — would have undone that consolidation. **Took option (b):** case 2's commit now delegates to `_finalize_stop_fill_locked`, and the card's own idempotency guard + Telegram were DELETED as redundant rather than left as a second copy (the canonical writer owns both, and also nulls `stop_order_id`). ✅ **The deadlock risk I flagged was checked, not assumed:** the lock is NOT re-entrant cross-connection, so the public wrapper would self-deadlock — it calls the `_locked` variant while holding the try-lock. **I verified independently that the `_locked` variant never acquires** (zero lock calls in its body). Gate now exit 0. Suite 6534. ▶ **VERIFY-LIVE = the next `sync_positions` sweep logs no `sync_position_gone_unresolved` for a healthy book, and any real occurrence Telegrams instead of silently closing.** Needs `deploy.sh execution` (broker/ runs there). [b1] >> ⏳ **EVENT-GATED, and I am NOT ticking it on an absence.** Checked prod 09-01: ZERO `sync_position_gone_unresolved` rows ever. That is CONSISTENT with the fix and PROVES nothing — the guarded path only runs when a position actually vanishes from the broker mid-flight, which has not happened. [[a-rule-is-not-live-until-it-has-fired-once]] **CLEARING CONDITION: the first real vanish event Telegrams instead of silently closing the row.** Until then it is unverifiable by construction, and re-dating monthly on a false 'looks fine' would be the fake-verify this board keeps paying for.

- #600 | 2026-09-05 | pending | ⚠ **The repair's place branch can re-arm a stop at a price the broker had already moved past — now reachable every 5 minutes instead of once a day.** Found 2026-08-26. `order_manager.py:5506-5510` calls `alpaca.place_stop_order(..., float(db_stop_price), ...)` directly, bypassing `update_stop`'s raise-only floor against broker truth (added 2026-08-10). The stale-price window has exactly three sources, all in `execute_partial_exit`'s breakeven-replace (`:3670`, `:3675-3730`, `:3817-3843`), which keep the stop pointer while withholding the price because the replace's outcome was unconfirmed. **Worst case is bounded at ~1R** (`breakeven_price − original_stop`, ≈$20-50 at today's sizing). Real-money analog for the same pathology via a different trigger: **FIGS 2026-08-07, $13.74 on 41 shares** (`docs/setups/exit_discipline.md`, 08-08 entry). ▶ **DoD: route the place branch through the same raise-only floor, or state why it must not be.** ⚠ Pre-existing mechanism — this week's retry did not create it, it raised the exposure from once at 16:05 to every 5 minutes for the life of a failed-repair episode. ⚖ Money path, `broker/` → `deploy.sh execution`. - #580 | 2026-09-30 | pending | **Theme strength must be reported WITH breadth, and the one-sided trim must be measured before it is trusted — the operator found `/theme` ranking biotech #2 while the dashboard ranked it #3 (2026-08-20) and ruled that neither surface is the answer: *"you named flaws in both… the solution is not one or the other, but a 3rd better option."* He is right.** 🔎 **THE TWO SURFACES DIVERGE FOR THREE REASONS, all confirmed in code:** (a) `/theme` (`agent.py:4822` → `briefing.py:549` `_compute_scored_themes`) RECOMPUTES from current constituent RS, while `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` reads the STORED `mi_themes.score` written by the previous nightly run; (b) `/theme` excludes Fading, the dashboard applies no Fading/Retired filter; (c) the dashboard ranks inside a WEEKLY bucket (`compute_weekly_rank`, `drop_duplicates(['week_start','name'], keep='last')`), so a Monday row can rank against a Thursday row. Verified against prod the same day: both produce an identical top-5 once 'latest row per name' is applied, so the Retired gap (#214) was NOT the active cause — freshness + Fading + bucketing are. 🚩 **THE DEFECT UNDERNEATH BOTH: a theme is reduced to ONE averaged number, and that average is computed by a ONE-SIDED trim.** `constants.trimmed_mean` sorts ascending and drops the lowest N (`sorted_vals[drop:]`) — the top is NEVER trimmed. Its docstring claims outlier resistance, but a real trimmed mean cuts both tails; this one only ever deletes losers, so every theme scores structurally high, always in the same direction. **At our actual basket sizes it is not 20%:** the live top themes hold 3, 4, 5 and 3 stocks (prod, 2026-08-19), so a 3-stock theme drops 1 of 3 — 33% — and the 'score' is a two-stock average. A theme carried by two names and a theme where eight of ten participate are indistinguishable. 📉 **BREADTH IS ALREADY COMPUTED AND ALREADY LOAD-BEARING — it is simply never surfaced.** `mi_themes.pct_above_20sma` is written by `theme_engine.py:3179/3338` and already FORCES a theme to Fading below `_BREADTH_DECAY_THRESHOLD` (`theme_engine.py:3285`). ⚠ **But it is NULL on 29 of 120 rows (2026-08-19) — including all four of the top-scoring themes.** The newest, hottest themes are exactly the ones with no breadth number, which is the population where it matters most; root-cause that before surfacing it, or the new column renders blank precisely when he looks. ✅ **BUILD (three pieces, in this order):** (1) **Surface breadth next to strength** on `/theme` and on the dashboard — small, the number already exists and is stored; (2) **fix the NULL-on-new-theme gap** so the top themes carry a breadth figure (`theme_engine.py:6233` sets None on the Retired path; find the new-theme path that does the same); (3) **MEASURE the trim before changing it** — do NOT edit it blind: `trimmed_mean` has SEVEN call sites across `briefing.py` (4), `theme_engine.py` (2 — including the engine's own `momentum`, which drives stage decisions) and `theme_ecosystems.py` (2), so a change moves engine stage transitions and ecosystem scores, not just a display. Produce a $0 read-only before/after on stored cohorts: score with trim vs plain mean vs both-tails trim, and report how much rank order actually moves. 🏗 **ONE COMPUTATION, TWO LABELLED SURFACES.** `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` is a standalone streamlit+psycopg2 process with NO imports from `agents/`, so 'shared' cannot mean a shared import — it means the engine WRITES the canonical number and both surfaces READ it, with `/theme` labelled 'where strength is now' and the dashboard labelled 'what the engine acted on'. Today they are two unlabelled rankings, which is why a disagreement reads as a bug. 🧭 **NORTH STAR:** subtle RS → early theme → buy before mainstream (memory `theme-north-star-early-rs-before-mainstream`). Breadth is what separates a real early theme from two stocks with a story — the exact discrimination the north star needs. ⛔ **NOT before the weekend EP work** (P10: EP profitability before new setups). **DoD:** `/theme` and the dashboard both show strength + breadth from the same stored number; no NULL breadth on any non-Retired theme; a written before/after showing what the trim was doing to rank order, with a ruling on whether it stays. ➕ **A THIRD DISAGREEING COUNT, found 2026-08-21 — same defect, new surface.** The L2 anomaly alert reported `theme_count_active = 159` against a 30d median of 122, while `mi_themes` holds **113 non-Retired rows for that date**. The alert counts DISTINCT NAMES seen in the last 7 days (`get_active_themes(stale_after_days=7)`); the table counts today's rows. Neither is wrong; they answer different questions and **both are labelled 'active themes'**, so the alert fires on a definitional gap rather than a real jump. ⚠ The underlying row count IS drifting up on its own (100 → 107 → 120 → 127 → 128 over five days) — that is worth a look, but it is not what the alert measured. ▶ Fold this surface into the same fix: one computation, each surface labelled with the question it answers. It was built to answer the bump chart's crossing-lines problem (#315 R2) and it does fix the crossings — but it fails for a different reason. 📊 **WHY, from the screenshot:** (a) almost all the ink is the BORING case — the thick horizontal ribbons are cohorts STAYING in their band — while promotions are hairline threads; (b) every ribbon is the same pale green, so nothing signals which move matters; (c) the band labels (Top 5 / 6-15 / 16-30 / Outside) render ON TOP of the ribbons and are half unreadable; (d) 4 bands x 11 weeks is far too many ribbons to trace one cohort through. ⚠ **THE REAL DEFECT IS NOT COSMETIC — it answers the wrong question.** It reports HOW MANY cohorts changed band. - #553 | 2026-09-18 | deployed | **Theme cohort-matching produces FALSE MERGES — a small theme inside a larger unrelated one is absorbed regardless of similarity** (found 2026-08-08 when the operator asked *"heatmap is the exact same as grid?"* — measuring that killed the heatmap view and exposed this underneath). ROOT CAUSE in `portfolio-app2/theme_data.py::dedup_themes`: the match test is CONTAINMENT of the smaller set (`|S ∩ L| / |S| >= 0.50`) with Jaccard used only as a TIE-BREAK, never as a gate. So a 2-3 member theme wholly inside a larger unrelated theme merges at containment 1.00 however different the cohorts are — and because merges CHAIN (A~B, B~C → A+B+C), unrelated themes collapse onto one identity. EVIDENCE (real snapshot): 'Satellite Mobile & IoT Connectivity' absorbed 'U.S. Defense Primes & Aerospace' (GD/LMT/NOC/RTX); 'Niche Specialty Chemicals' fused with 'IP Licensing & Ad-Tech Royalty Software'; a fertilizer→specialty-chem→engineered-polymer chain became one row. ⚠ **Rank Flow (live) is built on this identity** and inherits it — tolerable only because it aggregates into 4 rank bands, so a mis-key shifts a ribbon's thickness rather than mislabeling a row. Bump Chart + Forward Returns use it too, and Forward Returns' cohort counts depend on it. FIX DIRECTION (not yet decided): require BOTH a containment floor AND a Jaccard floor rather than containment alone; cap the size ratio on the subset path; and stop chain-merging (a merge target that has itself already absorbed a different cohort should not keep absorbing). ⚠ Same family as apollo-side theme churn (#476 name/member instability, #471 re-granularization) but this is the DASHBOARD's own matcher, a separate code path — do not conflate. DoD = the 3 evidenced false merges no longer merge + a regression test pinning each + the true-duplicate defense pair (Spending Surge / Contract Surge, both {PLTR,TSAT,VOYG,AMRC}) STILL merges + Rank Flow re-verified. [b0] >> ✅ **FIXED + PUSHED 2026-08-08** (operator: *"fix it"*). All 3 false merges verified gone by running the OLD and NEW matchers SIDE BY SIDE on the same snapshot (before=merged→after=separate on each); the true defence duplicate still merges. 34/311 → 23/332 merged cohorts. **3 mechanisms, only ONE was a suspect I handed over**: (a) satellite/defence was cross-day FIRST CONTACT — a brand-new name claiming a cohort it had never touched at Jaccard 0.667; (b) chemicals/ad-tech was the `min(min_shared, denom)` relaxation letting a 2-of-2 match through on a 2-ticker signature; (c) the chain was NOT one bad hop — every day-to-day step was individually plausible (0.7-1.0) while cumulative drift walked a 5-ticker cohort to an unrelated 15-ticker basket. FIXES: an **anchor set** (cohort freezes its tickers at creation; later matches must clear the bar against THAT, not just the drifted latest) — this is what actually stops chaining; a **0.70 first-contact floor** (board-wide ablation, sits between the bad 0.667 and genuine renames at 0.714/0.778); relaxation removed; `dedup_themes` gains an OPTIONAL `jaccard_floor` (default None). **GRID PROVEN UNCHANGED** — I re-verified the strongest check myself: `get_weekly_grid()` CSV sha256 identical before/after (ffea9cd2d93aea54). ⚠ My own first acceptance test was too coarse ('do these names ever share a cohort') and nearly rejected a correct fix — the remaining overlaps are genuine (83%, and near-identical 15-ticker sets). RESIDUAL left deliberately: a broad 'Oil & Gas' absorbs 3 same-day strict-subset sub-themes — a granularity loss, not a wrong-cohort error, and the same question #471 owns. - #545 | 2026-09-05 | pending | 🔬 **ENTRY/EXIT TACTICS PROGRAM — the EP cohort wins, our tactics do not (operator 2026-08-07, his framing and it is the sharpest statement of the problem so far).** *"EP stocks is a winning cohort overall (not high win rate, but major winners can be found here) however, entry/exit tactics is the big challenge… I want to run multiple variations of all the parameters including some more novel approaches like re-enter next day, which is similar to delayed EP, or even next few days when some delayed setup hits."* **WHY THIS IS A PROGRAM AND NOT A TASK:** we have tried pieces of this in isolation — same-day re-entry (R3, killed on 0-for-7), the 5-min ORB re-entry replay (`orb_5m_reentry_hybrid_replay`, never run), the giveback peak-lock (#306, built dark), the +2R partial (live 08-05), the stop-floor (tested and REFUTED 08-06) — each answered one axis on one cohort and none of them swept the space. The operator is asking for the sweep. **THE THESIS TO TEST, stated as he stated it:** a low-win-rate / fat-right-tail population is one you HARVEST, not one you filter harder — so the leverage is in WHEN you enter and HOW you hold, not in tightening what qualifies. **SCOPE (design first, then execute):** (a) inventory every entry/exit variant already tried, with what it showed and on which cohort — several are recorded across #306/#503/#508/#541 and nowhere collected; (b) the parameter space itself — entry timing (ORB 1m/5m · next-day open · N-day delayed setup · re-entry after stop-out), stop basis (ORB low · ADR multiple · structure), profit-take (fixed R · ADR · trail · none), and hold rules; (c) which cells the EXISTING replay harnesses can answer with data already captured vs which need new capture — `_306_intraday_partial_sim.py`, `_508_exit_rule_replay.py` and `_stop_floor_forward_replay.py` already do minute-bar replay with a frozen fill contract, so reuse them rather than minting a fourth; (d) a ranked read with the cost of each variant stated in R, not just the benefit. ⚠ **REGIME IS A FIRST-CLASS AXIS, not a footnote:** ~~all 14 closed live trades were taken in Correcting/Choppy/Crisis and ZERO in Bull (measured 08-06)~~ **← STALE, CORRECTED 2026-09-02: there are now 26 closed live trades and TEN are stamped Bull; the review ran (see the Phase 4 note at the end of this line) and the caveat is RE-WORDED, not retired**, so every conclusion we hold is a non-bull conclusion — see `exit_tune_bull_regime_read`. ⚠ **NOT a licence to flip anything:** this produces EVIDENCE and a ranked recommendation; every live change is CHANGE_PROCESS + #151 harness + operator sign-off (THE LINE). **DoD:** a design doc with the variant inventory, the parameter grid, the answerable-vs-needs-capture split, and a phased execution plan for the operator to sequence. ⚖ Operator-carried 08-07. Feeds/overlaps #306 (exit tune) and `exit_tune_cohort_review` (the n=20/40/60 accrual vehicle) — this is the SUPERSET they roll up into, not a duplicate; sequence it so their next runs execute inside this frame. >> 🔗 **WIRED TO ITS TRIGGERS 2026-08-07 — operator: *"we already have triggers to eval once we have more samples, are they linked? We should connect all the entry/exit analysis work."*** They were not; the reference was one-way and weak. Now both directions. **THE TWO ACCRUAL VEHICLES, which fire on their own and feed THIS task:** (1) `exit_tune_cohort_review` — recurring at n=20/40/60/80/100 closed live trades (today 14), carrying methods (a) per-trade forensic · (b) intraday partial sweep · (c0) regime segmentation · (c) character segmentation · (d) holding-period distribution, and now (e) stop geometry; (2) `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` — fires at 8 live closes in a BULL tape (today 0), owning the one cell nothing else can fill, since all 14 closed trades were non-bull. **THE DIVISION OF LABOUR:** the reviews are the SAMPLE CLOCK — they say WHEN there is enough data. #545 is the FRAME — it says WHICH variants are worth accruing toward, including the ones no trigger currently watches (next-day re-entry · N-day delayed setup · entry timing variants). Neither replaces the other, and both now say so in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` so a future run reports INTO this task rather than writing an orphan dated doc that #545 has to rediscover. ⚠ Deliberately NOT re-keyed onto a single trigger: a program that only wakes at n=20 cannot design its own grid, and the design work (the inventory + the parameter space) is answerable NOW from data already captured. >> 📌 **FIRST REAL INSTANCE OF THE DELAYED-ENTRY VARIANT — operator's own discretionary fill on TEAM, 2026-08-07, recorded while it is fresh and before the outcome is known.** He took the SAME stock Apollo was stopped out of, hours later, and is holding. **THE TWO ENTRIES SIDE BY SIDE, from prod + SIP bars:** Apollo — ORB entry $147.13 at 09:31, stop $143.21 (ORB low), **stopped out −$23.94** inside ~20 minutes. He bought into the third rising bar of that turn, not the low itself. **Day's range: high $151.37 at the open, low $141.51 at 13:55, last $148.42 at 17:45Z** — i.e. the stock round-tripped and then recovered above his entry. ⚖ **WHY THIS MATTERS TO #545 AND NOT JUST AS AN ANECDOTE:** it is the concrete form of the *next-day / delayed re-entry* variant this program was filed to test, and the operator's framing is that the EP COHORT is fine while the TACTICS are not — this is him demonstrating the tactic manually on the exact name the system rejected. **The measurable question it poses:** for every Day-1 stop-out, does a re-entry keyed on *base-then-turn* (rather than on the ORB) beat doing nothing? That is answerable OFFLINE on the recorded stop-out set — no new capture needed, `mi_intraday_bars` + the minute-bar harnesses already hold it. ⚠ **The hard part, stated honestly: "looking at the low forming, turn back up" is not yet a rule.** Before it can be swept it needs a mechanical proxy (e.g. N consecutive higher closes / higher lows off a session low, with a time window), and the proxy is a modelling CHOICE that will not be faithful to what he actually does. Also: MACD **stretch** (fast pulling away from slow) = extension, and its FLATTENING is his take-profit; the 20-EMA is the guide rail (resistance on a bounce from below, entry trigger on a pullback from above). ⚠ **TWO THINGS THE SOURCE SAYS THAT CONSTRAIN HOW WE TEST IT.** (1) **Parameter sensitivity is measured by the author himself** — he compares (6,20) / (8,24) / (12,26) and reports *the lower the setting, the earlier the signal AND the greater the chance it is false.* So periods are an AXIS in the sweep, not a constant. (2) **It is subordinate to price.** In his NVDA example the short entry came from the $400 Century Mark on price alone and the MACD cross was only confirmation — *"it was not necessary to use the MACD and/or moving averages to generate short-sale entries"*. The DoD's four parts are there: the variant inventory with cohort + n + whether the conclusion is load-bearing, the parameter grid, the answerable-now vs needs-capture split named per harness, and a ranked phased plan. ▶ **TOP 3, ALL $0:** (1) the RETRY test — stop width × up to 3 attempts, **per NAME at equal dollar risk** so every failed attempt is charged to the name (~1 session); (2) the MISSED-EP tail read across ALL FIVE skip buckets, not the one bucket ever read (~½ session, needs one read-only capture); (3) extend `ep_replay.py` with ADR stop modes + a second-attempt leg, then the joint day-1 stop × target × runner sweep (~1½ sessions). ⚠ **THREE OF THE BRIEF'S OWN PREMISES WERE WRONG and the card caught them** — verified independently in prod before recording: **(a) "ZERO closed live trades in Bull" IS STALE.** True at n=14 on 08-06; there are now **26 closed live trades and 10 are stamped Bull** (14 by regime-date join — the stamp and the join disagree on 7 rows, which is itself worth a look). **So `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` IS RIPE AND STILL `pending`: its own predicate (`account_mode='live' AND status='closed' AND regime='Bull'`) returns **10** against a threshold of **8**.** The "every conclusion we hold is a non-bull conclusion" caveat no longer holds and must stop being repeated. **(b) `orb_5m_reentry_hybrid_replay` was NOT never-run** — the review ran; its YAML entry was never closed. **(c) There are TEN replay lineages, not the four the line names**, and the campaign walker lives OUTSIDE the repo. ▶ **THE NUMBER THAT FRAMES THE WHOLE PROGRAM, verified in prod today: of 26 closed live trades, 5 are winners, the best is +3.31R (PLTR 2026-08-04), the worst −1.18R, and NONE has ever reached 4R.** ⚠ Only 2 of the 26 carry `risk_dollars_actual`, so R must be computed over `COALESCE(risk_dollars_actual, risk_dollars)` — computing it on the actual-only rows gives a best of +0.50R and is a 2-row sample wearing a 26-row headline. ⚖ Evidence + a ranked recommendation only; every live change is CHANGE_PROCESS + #151 + sign-off. >> 📦 **PHASE 2's CAPTURE IS DONE (2026-09-02, read-only, `scripts/probes/_545p2_capture.sql` → `_545p2_out.txt`) — and doing it myself caught TWO TRAPS that would have produced a confidently wrong answer.** ⚠ **(1) UNIT: `ret_1d/5d/20d` ARE FRACTIONS, NOT PERCENTS** (`missed_outcomes.py:652` computes `(close_d20 - open_d0) / open_d0`). My first run asked for `ret_20d >= 20` — i.e. +2000% — and returned **ZERO tail names in every one of the 20 buckets with a mean of 0.0**, which reads as *no skipped EP has ever run* and is false on its face. Re-run at `>= 0.20` the same buckets show **tail shares of 15–22% and best returns of +70% to +170%**. The SQL now states the unit in its header. ⚠ **(2) RIGHT-CENSORING, and it hits exactly the period we care about: 507 of 1,419 gapped rows have NO `ret_20d`, and 382 of those are AUGUST** — the 20-day window has not closed. The tail read covers 41 of 423 August rows (10%). **HTFL — the case he raised — is in the table twice (`duplicate_scan` AND `stop_too_wide`), `setup_at_open = true`, `ret_5d = +27.4%`, and `ret_20d` NULL — so the ret_20d tail filter SILENTLY EXCLUDES THE PROMPTING CASE.** Phase 2 must read `ret_5d` for recent rows or state the censoring; a pure ret_20d read is a pre-August read wearing a current headline. ▶ First-cut buckets by tail share (≥+20% at 20d, gapped-at-open only): `session_rvol_low` 29/129 · `outside_top20` 34/174 · `mcap_low` 13/62 · `adv_low` 12/61 · `duplicate_scan` 18/90. ⚠ Those are DAILY-GRAIN PROXIES for the tail, NOT realized R — most of these names would have met a day-1 stop first, which is the second half of the phase and is not yet done. Do not quote the tail share as an edge. >> ⏸ **PHASE 1 (the retry test) STARTED 2026-09-02 ~14:25 PDT AND WAS KILLED MID-FLIGHT by the rolling session cap (resets 16:50 PDT) — NOTHING WAS WRITTEN.** The card is RESUMABLE with its context intact; resume it rather than re-scoping, and do not re-run what it already did. ▶ **The one thing it had established before it died: `NRIX` and `EFOR` are the two names carrying the headline cell**, and it was about to hand-walk them in raw bars so the result is checkable — do that check, it is the difference between a number and a claim. ⚠ **This is the second kill by the 5-hour cap in two days** (three Fable cards died 09-01). The preceding card on this same task cost 486k subagent tokens; that is what left no room. Budget: ONE heavy card per window, and say the budget out loud at the start of it. >> ✅ **THE 0.25×ADR STOP IS NOW RECORDED PROSPECTIVELY (Sonnet card, 2026-09-02, operator-approved *"go with rec on both"*)** — a third shadow variant beside #616's 0.75/1.00, same mechanism, same guarded per-suffix write, incumbent byte-identity re-proven with 0/2/3 variants present. Ships in the same deploy as #616, so prod never sees a two-variant window. 🔴 **BUT THE CARD FOUND A LIMITATION THAT PARTLY UNDOES MY OWN RECOMMENDATION, and it is right:** **this recording CANNOT reconstruct the retry chain forward.** `get_delayed_entry_reentry_candidates` selects FIRST-attempt triggers settled as **`outcome='stop'`** — the INCUMBENT's outcome — so a campaign where the incumbent survives but a 0.25×ADR stop WOULD have been hit generates no re-entry row for that counterfactual. What accrues is the **first-attempt-only** leg of 0.25×ADR — and #545's own backfill shows that leg is a **LOSER alone: −32.7R on the trail arm ex-May**. The +54.4R came from the CHAIN (up to 3 tries), not from the tight stop by itself. ⚠ **So a future reader must not read a negative single-shot 0.25 result as refuting the retry candidate, nor sum rows across `reentry_shape` and call that the chain** — the new registry entry `delayed_entry_adr_stop_variant_025_545` says exactly this in its question and its `action_when_ready`, deliberately kept SEPARATE from #616's review rather than folded in. ▶ **WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY TEST HIS IDEA FORWARD is a re-entry lane keyed on the VARIANT's stop, not the incumbent's — not built, not scoped, and a bigger change than this one.** >> ✅ **PHASE 4 RUN 2026-09-02 — `exit_tune_bull_regime_read`, the gate that was ripe and silent, at stamped-Bull n=10** (`docs/analysis/exit_tune_bull_regime_read_2026-09-02.md`). ▶ **THE ANSWER SPLITS IN TWO, which is why the caveat is re-worded rather than retired: the STOP conclusions HOLD in Bull** — 7 of 10 Bull trades died on the entry day and the ORB-low stop sits at a median 0.42 of a single day's range, 7 of 7 under one day, i.e. tape-independent. **The HARVEST-side conclusions (partial, breakeven-trail, stop-floor) CANNOT BE GRADED**: every Bull trade ran the post-08-05 exit stack and every non-Bull trade except CRWD ran bare, so a Bull-vs-non-Bull difference here is a STACK contrast, not a TAPE contrast (era-matched cell is 3 vs 1). Bull −1.95R / 3 winners vs non-Bull −9.33R / 2. ▶ **THE REGIME BASIS QUESTION IS SETTLED, and my own number was the wrong one:** the trade's `regime` STAMP is the PRIOR session's regime — verified in code, `broker/live_tracker.py:523` reads `regime_date <= $1 ORDER BY regime_date DESC LIMIT 1` at 09:31 while that day's row is not written until the 17:00 ET pull — so the stamp is knowable at entry and **the date-join I quoted (14 Bull) is LOOK-AHEAD. Ten is the honest number.** The disagreement is 8 rows, not 7. ▶ **R BASIS CORRECTED TOO:** `COALESCE(risk_dollars_actual, risk_dollars)` is the PRE-CAP budget on 24 of 26 rows; on the recorder's realized per-share R the cohort is **best PLTR +3.42R, worst BW −1.09R, total −11.27R, and still NONE ≥4R realized** (3 reached ≥4R unrealized). ▶ **Trigger re-keyed** to `LEAST(Bull-stamped, 4 × non-Bull era-C) ≥ 20` so it cannot fire again on a pure stack contrast; status stays `pending` because the entry is RECURRING by its own text (8→20→40), with a run note recorded on it. ⚠ Not run: the 34-candidate grid — the 08-17/08-22 engine snapshots lived in session scratchpads and are GONE; a capture for run 2 is written. >> 🛑 **OPERATOR CAUGHT ME RE-MEASURING THE STOP HE ALREADY WIDENED (2026-09-02): *"you said widen stop but we already did exactly that with 2R update recently"*. He is right, and this is the SECOND time this exact error has been made on this cohort** — `exit_tune_cohort_review`'s own comment records the first: *"the n=20 run on 2026-08-22 fired on a 22-trade record in which 21 trades ran under exit rules that had ALREADY been replaced — it re-measured a stop the operator had widened on 08-16 and told him the old one was too tight. Redundant by construction."* My *"7 of 10 Bull trades died on the entry day"* is dominated by PRE-2R trades (the Bull cohort is era B×7 + C×3), so it measured the OLD stop and I presented it as a live problem. ▶ **THE ACTUAL SPLIT, measured in prod tonight:** **pre-2R — 22 closed, 16 died on entry day (73%), 3 winners, −$150.49** · **post-2R (era C) — 4 closed, 1 died on entry day (25%), 2 winners, −$15.16.** ▶ **So the widening is directionally doing exactly what it was meant to do, and n=4 means it proves nothing yet.** ⚖ **THEREFORE THE RECOMMENDATION IS TO CHANGE NOTHING AND LET IT ACCRUE** — `exit_tune_cohort_review` fires at 20 era-C closes (4 today). Any stop conclusion drawn before then is a conclusion about a stop we no longer use. 🔒 **STANDING RULE FROM THIS: before reporting any exit/stop finding, SPLIT ON THE ERA FIRST and say the era-C n out loud.** A pooled number on this cohort is a pre-change number wearing a current headline. >> 🔑 **PHASE 2 RUN AND RESOLVED 2026-09-02 — AND IT INVERTS THE QUESTION: no skip bucket is losing us the tail; the BRACKET is.** `docs/analysis/545p2_missed_ep_tail_read_2026-09-02.md`, reader `scripts/probes/_545p2_read.py` (re-runnable, $0), capture 2 run. ▶ **THE NUMBER THAT SETTLES IT — the tail is ALREADY IN WHAT WE ADMIT, and we do not convert it:** admitted alerts reach +20% at 20 sessions on **13.8% of HIGHs · 13.2% of all 270 · 10.7% gapped-at-open**, and the 26 closed LIVE trades hit the same proxy at **13.3%**. **But of the proxy-tail names the bracket actually entered, it realized ≥4R on ZERO** (live: 2 tail names → 0 at ≥4R, 1 at ≥2R; paper: 2 → 0 and 0). Live cohort realized: **sum −7.8R, best +3.31R, ≥4R = 0, 21 of 26 losers.** ▶ **So the skip buckets at 19–22% are NOT dramatically richer than the 13% we already admit** — and part of even that excess is artefact: **46 of 238 tail rows are non-common-stock or pre-04-20**, before the security-type filter landed, with `session_rvol_low` the worst (26 of 34 tails pre-filter) and `outside_top20` next (15 of 42). ⚠ **My earlier "15–22% of the EPs we refuse later ran +20%" was missing that comparator and that contamination — the honest read is that they are ROUGHLY THE SAME RATE as what we admit.** ▶ **The one bucket that clears the bar is `stop_too_wide`** (13 rejects on record, not 9; BAND +110% and STRL +32%, both pre-05-11 and not replayable) — and it is exactly the operator's own *"size down, not skip"* cell. **HTFL, the case that started this, DOES enter under the current bracket and pays +1.28R (harness) / +1.43R (live path) — a winner, not a tail.** ⚖ **CONCLUSION, and it matches his own thesis (*harvest a fat-tailed population, do not filter it harder*): the binding constraint is CONVERSION, not admission.** Adding supply cannot help while we convert none of the tail we already have. 🐛 **Found in passing: `ep_replay.py` books a day-3/5 ladder partial that LIVE stands down** (`live_tracker.py:1076`) — moves 14 of 267 campaigns, flips CORT/ATRO from small wins to small losses; no verdict changed, but it belongs in the harness's KNOWN DEVIATIONS. >> 🔑 **PHASE 3 RUN 2026-09-03 — AND THE DAY-1 ANSWER IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE DAY-2+ BAND: on the names TODAY'S SCORER ADMITS, TIGHTER WINS.** `docs/analysis/545p3_day1_stop_target_runner_sweep_2026-09-03.md`. Harness `validate` re-run and still PASS, identical to the 09-01 baseline (44/44 · 33/33 · 29/30 · 25/30); suite 6899. ▶ **THE CELL:** **0.5×ADR with the target PINNED beats the live `entry−2R` stop by +14.2R paired on 55 admitted campaigns**, ex-May +14.1R vs −2.6R, four ≥4R (HTFL, PLTR, ABCL, ARGX) against NONE, +4.3 in size-free ADR units. Pessimistic bound once the names live's `stop_too_wide` gate refuses are removed: **+0.5R — positive on every pre-registered criterion, bound AT zero.** **ORB low is the more robust of the two: +11.4R paired, bound +5.0R, +3.9R even ex-gate** — and it CONFLICTS with the 08-16 read it was retired on (different population: 43 reconstructed Apr–May trades). Surfaced, not resolved. **0.75×ADR marginal; 1.0 and 1.25×ADR — the day-2+ band — FAIL on both populations.** ▶ **WHY EVERY EARLIER READ SAID THE OPPOSITE, and it is P8 in one line: POOLED over all 267 alerts the live stop looks best (+11.3R) — and that entire margin belongs to names the current scorer REJECTS (+13.1R on 21, AMBQ +9.3R alone). On the 65 it ADMITS the live stop is +3.5R with ZERO ≥4R and −2.6R ex-May.** A stop conclusion drawn on a population we no longer trade is a conclusion about a different system. ▶ **PREREQUISITE 2 ANSWERED — DID THE 08-16 WIDENING WORK? PARTLY, and not the way the live n=4 suggested.** Era-matched, replaying the 22 pre-2R live trades under the current stack: entry-day deaths **16 → 12 of 22 (73% → 55%)**, **+2R at equal risk** — but it got there by turning six deaths into five +0.33R scratches and one +1.1R, **and halving PLTR/ABCL/ETON's R**. The live 25% (n=4) sits inside the noise of 55%. ▶ **NOTHING ELSE PASSES:** the target-moving frame reproduces the 08-06 mechanism exactly (partials 54→32, every wide stop worsens); all 13 runner rules are 3-name-carried with taker win-rate 100%→61-76%; attempt 2 FAILS at the live stop on all three signals and is THC-alone at a tight one. ▶ **All three legacy harnesses (#2, #5, #8) reproduce their headlines and CAN NOW RETIRE.** ⚖ **THE FORK IS HIS (Phase 6): 0.5×ADR or ORB-low as a CHANGE_PROCESS candidate** — sign-off + backtest + the #151 harness. The next cheapest evidence is a #616-style counterfactual-stop recorder on LIVE fills. **Nothing flipped.** ⚠ Corrections the card made to the design doc: 267 campaigns not 270 (three double-inserts); 2 open-at-horizon not 87 (85 are alerts detected ≥09:45 the ORB window never submits); BAND/STRL were never live-source alerts and have no bars in any capture, so `stop_too_wide`'s Phase 2 verdict should read n=0 replayed, not 2 tail names.

- #313 | 2026-10-27 | pending | P6 Institution-grade ops (H1→H2): #256 split (done) + replay-everything CI (#302) + cost governance (per-role LLM spend vs P&L) [b3] [ok:#419 Phase-2 milestone re-date, operator-signed 7/5] >> ✅ **COST-GOVERNANCE INCREMENT SHIPPED 2026-08-02 — spend is now ATTRIBUTABLE PER LANE, and a lane that switches on ANNOUNCES ITSELF.** Operator, after the chart-vision shadow ran unnoticed: *"we can have hidden costs that provides no value that is running and no way for us to know about in time."* ⚠ **THE COST STACK WAS NOT THE FAILURE** — the #377 meter, the #378 board/alarm and the #379 per-caller anomaly + reduction detectors all worked; they were reading a bucket with TWO THINGS IN IT. `grade_holistic`'s `log_caller` had a **DEFAULT** of `ep_grade_judge`; the chart shadow took it, and **336 of the 379 calls in that bucket over 30 days ($12.90) were an experiment indistinguishable from live grading.** The dollars were always derivable — the ATTRIBUTION was not. `judge_divergence` had overridden the default correctly and the docstring explained why, so convention existed, was documented, and the next lane still didn't follow it. **(1) The default is GONE** — `log_caller` is REQUIRED; every lane names itself. The sweep found **8 unlabelled call sites, not 1** — every offline eval and replay was ALSO billing the live judge (`tape_judge_eval`, `judge_model_eval`, `judge_enrich_eval`, `judge_backfill_replay`, `selection_replay_268`, `judge_integration_probe`, `chart_axis_shadow`, `chart_axis_eval` are now distinct buckets). **(2) NEW-LANE DETECTOR** (`cost_board._new_lanes_from_rows` + the 17:52 ET watchdog): a caller with spend in the last 3d and ZERO across the trailing 30d Telegrams ONCE, ever (audit-log dedupe, the same idiom the 7/17 budget-alarm re-fire fix used). This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution. [b3] [ok:operator-CLOSE reconcile 7/17 — verify-live pending Monday market / remaining-deliverable / event-gated] >> 7/27 DECISION FRAMING added 7/19 (operator premortem walk; stage-2 design doc §'7/27 decision framing'): **family cap = PRIMARY (selective — allows a diversified 5, blocks concentrated)**; the 'family-blind notional axis' is DROPPED (for Apollo's ~uniform 20% sizing it collapses to a count cap → if a blunt total-exposure ceiling is wanted, just trim MAX_CONCURRENT_LIVE_POSITIONS); the trim is a SECONDARY/optional backstop for the un-themed correlated book ONLY. **7/27 = two questions: (1) family KEY (stem vs ECOSYSTEM — operator lean, closes the same-sector leak) + (2) add the MAX_POSITIONS blunt backstop?** Replay: (a)-(d) per-granularity + (e) MAX_POSITIONS-trim bind/cost + (f) worst un-themed-correlated exposure the family cap misses. >> 📏 **MEASURED + THRESHOLD LOWERED 2→1 (operator-ruled 2026-07-27). The Stage-2 BLOCKING fork is NOT taken — deliberately, on evidence.** The shadow had recorded **ZERO** `exposure_family_breach` events since it shipped 7/12, and that zero is HONEST, not broken instrumentation — I checked the acting population per [[shadow-zero-effect-check-instrumentation]]. **The live book has never been full:** 2026-05-01→07-27, on days holding anything — max concurrent **4** (once), **average 1.50**, only **4 days** with 2+, **1 day** with 3+. At threshold 2 an emit needs two EXISTING same-family positions plus the candidate = three of a book that has exceeded two positions twice ever. **So the premortem R1 risk ('5 same-family EP HIGHs fill all 5 slots = one bet at ~100% deployment') is real in principle and UNOBSERVED in practice — the binding constraint today is book SIZE, not correlation.** Shipping a blocking cap now would guard a zero-instance risk while adding a new failure mode to the entry path; that fork was explicitly declined. At threshold 1 an emit means 'this entry makes TWO positions share a theme' — the base rate that must exist before a cap can be argued for at all. Still SHADOW (entry_pipeline stage 2b, error-wrapped #94 pattern, can never block). Test that pinned the OLD intent was rewritten to pin the new one, plus a new floor test that zero-same-family still never emits (guards against dropping to 0 and alerting on every entry). Suite 3821. [ok:operator-ruled data-accrual window — the cap decision needs a base rate that does not exist yet, 2026-07-27→2026-08-11] **NEW ETA 2026-08-11 = revisit with ~2 weeks of threshold-1 data; the cap decision needs a base rate we do not yet have.** >> 🔎 **LIKELY-BUILT SWEEP 2026-07-31 — checked, classification is HONEST, no change.** The surface flags this line because it CONTAINS a built/deployed marker, not because the task is done: Stage-1 telemetry shipped to shadow; the operator ruled a data-accrual window to 8/11 because the cap decision needs a base rate that does not exist yet. >> 🔧 **STATUS CORRECTED 2026-08-09 — `in_progress` hid a real pending verify.** The hook IS shipped; what remains is confirmation. ⚠ **AND THE CONFIRMATION HAS NEVER HAPPENED: zero correlated-book rows exist in `mi_audit_log`, ever** — checked on prod today. [[a-rule-is-not-live-until-it-has-fired-once]]: shipped + green is not evidence it can ACT. **VERIFY-DUE 2026-08-11** = the hook logs on a real entry evaluation (needs a market day; Monday is the first). If Monday passes with entries evaluated and still zero rows, the hook is INERT and that is a bug, not a wait. >> 🔍 **REVIEWED 2026-08-11 — verdict TOO THIN, do NOT promote; and this line's own verify framing was WRONG.** It said *entries evaluated and still zero rows ⇒ the hook is INERT, a bug*. **Zero rows was the CORRECT output.** All-time `exposure_family_breach` count is 0, and that zero was proven honest two ways: a replay of ~37 stage-2b check moments against open-trade intervals + as-of-day theme membership said 'no breach' correctly every time, and a read-only probe of the LIVE code inside apollo-execution returns `breach=True, same_family=['PLTR']` for a hypothetical VOYG — a TRUE family. The detector fires, and correctly. ✅ #553's false-merge window does NOT contaminate this: the hook keys on trailing-7-day ACTIVE snapshots, so the sample read `theme_date` 07-05…08-09, entirely after the mid-April fixes. 🔴 **THE TWO FINDINGS THAT MATTER MORE THAN THE REVIEW QUESTION — the premortem's TOP risk already happened, in miniature, undetected.** **(1) SAME-DAY THEME-BIRTH BLINDNESS:** `mi_themes` date-D rows are written by the 17:05 ET nightly, so a 09:31 check sees only ≤ D-1. **Verified on 2026-08-04: PLTR filled and VOYG was evaluated in the SAME SECOND (both 09:31:11) — and the two defense themes holding {PLTR,TSAT,VOYG,AMRC} were first written with `theme_date=2026-08-04`, i.e. that EVENING.** The key could not see the family. VOYG was kept out of the book by `setup:zero_range: open=high=low=$33.99` — a bar glitch — NOT by any correlation control. **(2) COVERAGE ~22%:** only ~8 of ~37 evaluated candidates had ANY active-theme membership at check time; SMCI, the archetypal AI-hardware name, was in no active theme 07-22→07-27 while it was the largest open position. **A BLOCKING cap on this key would be blind to ~78% of entries** — which is the argument against promoting it, independent of the zero. ⚖ **OPERATOR'S CALL (THE LINE — nothing promoted, no toggle touched):** keep SHADOW observing and re-gate on EVENTS not the calendar — successor registry entry `exposure_family_cap_promotion_r2` fires at ≥3 real breach rows, each to be judged true-vs-false family. ▶ The coverage + day-lag gaps point at the ecosystem-key fork already in `docs/analysis/452_r1_stage2_design_2026-07-17.md` (operator lean 7/19) as the PREREQUISITE for any cap worth having — a cap keyed on same-day theme membership cannot work while themes are born after the close. [ok:2026-08-11→2026-09-01 — the review RAN and returned a verdict; the re-date carries only the r2 event gate, and the wrong 'inert ⇒ bug' framing is corrected above rather than left to mislead the next reader] >> 📊 **32 NIGHTS READ 2026-09-01 — THE RISK HAS NOT MATERIALISED, AND THE INSTRUMENT CAN SEE IT.** `book_concentration_snapshot` has fired every night since 7/17: **32 nights, ZERO flagged families, every single one.** The entry-path shadow hook has **never fired once** — zero `exposure_family_breach` rows, ever. ⚠ **CHECKED FOR THE FALSE ZERO before drawing any conclusion** [[shadow-zero-effect-check-instrumentation]]: the family key (active-theme membership) CAN match — **22 of the 28 tickers we have ever held carry theme membership**, so this is a real zero, not a dark detector. 🔑 **THE REASON IS SIMPLER THAN ANY GATE: we almost never hold enough positions for it to bind.** Nights by open-position count: 0 open ×8 · 1 ×7 · 2 ×11 · 3 ×4 · 4 ×2 — **we have NEVER held 5**, and a >2-per-family breach needs three in one family, which only 6 of 32 nights could even reach. Notional runs $695-$1,832 (14-37% of equity), never the ~100%-deployed one-bet the premortem feared. ▶ **REC: do NOT build Stage 2 now.** It is a safeguard against a shape our trade frequency currently prevents, and it would be our first entry-path gate built with zero observed instances. **Re-open it on the EVENT, not a date: the first night we hold 5 concurrent live positions, or the first `exposure_family_breach`** — Stage 1 is already watching for both and Telegrams on the breach. ⚖ Stage 2 is entry discipline = THE LINE; this is a recommendation, his call.
